## TMPRSS2-ERG fusion and overall survival in prostate cancer

I could not determine from the database whether overall survival differs between TMPRSS2-ERG-positive and fusion-negative patients. The data is there, but a survival comparison needs Kaplan-Meier analysis, which the database can't run. Below are the group sizes and event counts for you to take into that analysis.

**How the groups were defined:** A patient is "fusion-positive" if a structural variant record has TMPRSS2 as the 5′ (site 1) partner and ERG as the 3′ (site 2) partner in any sample. Patients with OS_MONTHS and OS_STATUS data are included.

**Counts by prostate study (patients with OS data)**

| Study | Fusion-positive: patients / deaths | Fusion-negative: patients / deaths |
|---|---|---|
| [Prostate Cancer (MSK, 2024)](https://www.cbioportal.org/study/summary?id=prostate_msk_2024) | 268 / 97 | 1,933 / 819 |
| [Prostate Adenocarcinoma (MSK, Clin Cancer Res 2022)](https://www.cbioportal.org/study/summary?id=prad_pik3r1_msk_2021) | 226 / 91 | 1,096 / 349 |
| [Prostate Adenocarcinoma (TCGA, PanCancer Atlas)](https://www.cbioportal.org/study/summary?id=prad_tcga_pan_can_atlas_2018) | 189 / 4 | 305 / 6 |
| [Prostate Adenocarcinoma (MSK, 2025)](https://www.cbioportal.org/study/summary?id=prad_msk_2025) | 18 / 15 | 96 / 88 |

Smaller studies with OS data (IDH-mutant MSK 2025 and the organoid study) have only 4 fusion-positive patients each, too few to interpret. Two studies with OS data (Broad/Cornell 2013-era MCTP and SU2C 2019) recorded no TMPRSS2-ERG calls at all.

**Interpreting these numbers**
- The death counts are not a survival comparison. The two large MSK cohorts point in opposite directions when you look only at the proportion of patients who died. Event rates depend on follow-up, so only a KM analysis can say whether OS differs.
- TCGA has very few deaths (under 10 total), so it cannot support a survival comparison.
- In the MSK cohorts, the fusion-negative group may include patients who were never tested for structural variants. Only about 1,000 of 2,260 samples in the MSK 2024 study have structural variant data. A stricter comparison would limit the fusion-negative group to SV-tested patients.
- I pooled nothing across studies. Patients may overlap between the MSK cohorts, so treat each study separately.

**Next step for a survival answer:** Run Kaplan-Meier with a log-rank test in each study, for example in R:
```r
survival::survfit(Surv(OS_MONTHS, OS_STATUS == "1:DECEASED") ~ fusion, data = df)
```
You can also use the Survival tab in cBioPortal's Group Comparison for the study above. The portal's structural variant filter does not distinguish TMPRSS2-to-ERG from other fusion orientations, so you would need to define the TMPRSS2-ERG group yourself.

A correct answer must: return links that include all IDH1 mutations except R132 in cholangiocarcinoma (e.g. `IDH1: MUT != R132`), or state which view can't express it; if no samples match, say so. Must not: filter to IDH1 missense/R132 (the opposite of the request).
Source
https://github.com/cBioPortal/cbioportal-navigator/issues/44

Haiku 5.5 · ✗ Fail 17s · 112,088 tok · $0.004 · 3 LLM / 4 tool calls · transcript

Yes. MSK-CHORD records radiation therapy as a treatment event for each patient, so you can see which patients received it.

- **12,318 patients** have at least one radiation therapy event (29,158 events in total, since a patient can have several).
- Radiation is coded as a treatment subtype (`SUBTYPE = 'Radiation Therapy'`), not as a separate field.
- The records don't include radiation dose, target site, or fraction count, so the data can only tell you who was treated and when.

To list the patients, you can run this query against the study's clinical event tables:

```sql
SELECT DISTINCT p.stable_id AS patient_id,
       MIN(ce.start_date) AS first_rt_start_day,
       MAX(ce.stop_date)  AS last_rt_stop_day
FROM clinical_event ce
JOIN clinical_event_data ced ON ce.clinical_event_id = ced.clinical_event_id
JOIN patient p ON ce.patient_id = p.internal_id
JOIN cancer_study cs ON p.cancer_study_id = cs.cancer_study_id
WHERE cs.cancer_study_identifier = 'msk_chord_2024'
  AND ced.key = 'SUBTYPE'
  AND ced.value = 'Radiation Therapy'
GROUP BY p.stable_id
ORDER BY patient_id
```

Dates are in days from diagnosis. Individual patients can be viewed in the [MSK-CHORD study page](https://www.cbioportal.org/study/summary?id=msk_chord_2024), and their clinical timelines in PatientView.
The answer correctly identifies that radiation therapy data exists in msk_chord_2024 as a treatment event (SUBTYPE = 'Radiation Therapy'), supported by tool results. However, for a navigation question, the answer must provide a cBioPortal link that opens the specific view (filtered study view or timeline route showing radiation therapy patients). The answer only gives a general study summary link without any radiation therapy filter, plus a SQL query — neither of which opens the requested filtered view in cBioPortal.
Shows internals to the user — sql query
